========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, hello doctor. It’s nice to meet you too. I... well, my daughter actually drove me here today, which was lovely of her, though we got stuck in that awful traffic over on Elm Street. They’ve been doing construction there since, oh, it must be April. The detour signs are just terribly confusing. 

But anyway, what brings me in... well, I suppose for the past year or so, I just feel like I’m always puttering around. Fixing things, arranging the cupboards, volunteering for just one more committee at the church... and even at 87, I just have a terribly hard time sitting down and being still. 

Like yesterday, I was supposed to be resting and watching my afternoon programs, but I noticed the hinge on the back screen door was squeaking. So I went out to the garage to find the oil, and while I was out there I saw the petunias needed deadheading, and then I found myself reorganizing the entire shelf where my late husband kept his gardening supplies. He always loved his tomatoes, you know. He would spend hours out there in the summer, and we’d have more tomatoes than we knew what to do with, so I’d always be canning them and giving them to the neighbors...

Oh, heavens no. I still need my sleep, just like always. By the time evening rolls around, I'm usually quite ready for bed. I have this wonderful mattress, you see. We bought it... oh, it must have been fifteen years ago now, from the Sears over at the mall. 

It's such a shame what happened to Sears, isn't it? I used to buy all my daughter's school clothes there when she was little. You could get those sturdy corduroy pants that would last through absolutely everything, not like the flimsy things they sell nowadays. And their appliances! My sister Martha had a Kenmore washing machine that lasted her thirty years. Thirty years! Can you imagine? Now you buy a toaster and you're lucky if it makes it to Christmas. 

But... oh, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, yes, my sleep. No, I don't need less sleep at all. I sleep just the normal amount, I'd say.

Memory? Oh, no, I don't think so. My memory is sharp as a tack, thankfully. Why, just yesterday I was telling my daughter about the time we went to the World's Fair in New York... that was 1964, you know. We took the train, and I remember exactly what I was wearing—a lovely blue linen dress with white piping. 

You just don't see tailoring like that anymore. Everything nowadays is made of that stretchy material and it just doesn't breathe at all in the summer heat. We had a terrible heatwave that year, the year of the Fair. My husband bought one of those big metal box fans for the living room window. It was so heavy, and it was so terribly loud you had to shout over it just to hear the television! We used to watch Walter Cronkite every evening with that fan roaring in the background. He had such a trustworthy face, didn't he? Not like the news anchors today, with all that shouting and the flashing graphics at the bottom of the screen. It just makes me feel so jittery watching it, so I usually just turn the set off and go find a closet to reorganize. 

But... oh, what was the question? My memory? Yes, no, my memory is perfectly fine, I remember everything just like it was yesterday.

S014\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. The detour signs are just terribly confusing. \n\nBut anyway, what brings me in... well, I suppose for the past year or so, I just feel like I’m always puttering around. Fixing things, arranging the cupboards, volunteering for just one more committee at the church... and even at 87, I just have a terribly hard time sitting down and being still. \n\nLike yesterday, I was supposed to be resting and watching my afternoon programs, but I noticed the hinge on the back screen door was squeaking. So I went out to the garage to find the oil, and while I was out there I saw the petunias needed deadheading, and then I found myself reorganizing the entire shelf where my late husband kept his gardening supplies. He always loved his tomatoes, you know. He would spend hours out there in the summer, and we’d have more tomatoes than we knew what to do with, so I’d always be canning them and giving them to the neighbors...\n\nDoctor: When you're in these busy spells, do you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. Everything nowadays is made of that stretchy material and it just doesn't breathe at all in the summer heat. We had a terrible heatwave that year, the year of the Fair. My husband bought one of those big metal box fans for the living room window. It was so heavy, and it was so terribly loud you had to shout over it just to hear the television! We used to watch Walter Cronkite every evening with that fan roaring in the background. He had such a trustworthy face, didn't he? Not like the news anchors today, with all that shouting and the flashing graphics at the bottom of the screen. It just makes me feel so jittery watching it, so I usually just turn the set off and go find a closet to reorganize. \n\nBut... oh, what was the question? My memory? Oh, recent things? No, no, I wouldn't say so. I remember what I had for breakfast this morning—a lovely bowl of oatmeal with just a touch of brown sugar. My daughter bought me this new kind of brown sugar, it comes in a resealable pouch which is just marvelous.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Bills? Oh, no, I don't have any trouble with those. My daughter Sarah comes over every other Tuesday and we sit at the kitchen table and go through the mail together. She brings these lovely little pastries from that bakery down on Main Street... the one that used to be the old hardware store? 

Mr. Henderson ran that hardware store for fifty years, I swear. He always had a jar of peppermint candies on the counter for the children. Real peppermint, you know, the kind that would make your tongue tingle. You just can't find candies like that anymore. Everything now is all artificial flavors and colors. Why, I bought a bag of lemon drops last week and they were bright neon yellow! Like a highlighter pen! I couldn't bring myself to eat them, so I put them out in a dish for the mailman, but he hasn't touched them either. Poor man, he's always rushing these days. They give them these enormous routes now, not like when Henry used to deliver our mail. Henry knew everyone's name, and he'd always stop to ask about my hydrangeas. 

Oh, but... what were we talking about? The bills? Yes, no trouble at all. Sarah makes sure everything gets paid right on time.

He always had a jar of peppermint candies on the counter for the children. Real peppermint, you know, the kind that would make your tongue tingle. You just can't find candies like that anymore. Everything now is all artificial flavors and colors. Why, I bought a bag of lemon drops last week and they were bright neon yellow! Like a highlighter pen! I couldn't bring myself to eat them, so I put them out in a dish for the mailman, but he hasn't touched them either. Poor man, he's always rushing these days. They give them these enormous routes now, not like when Henry used to deliver our mail. Henry knew everyone's name, and he'd always stop to ask about my hydrangeas. \n\nOh, but... what were we talking about? The bills? Yes, no trouble at all. Sarah makes sure everything gets paid right on time.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhat year is it right now?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. The year? Oh, goodness, the years just fly by so fast now, don't they? I was just looking at the new calendar Sarah hung up in the kitchen for me. She always gets me the ones with the baby animals. This one has the most precious little kittens on it. 

I had a kitten once, a little tuxedo cat named Sylvester. We found him under the porch during a terrible thunderstorm. My husband had to crawl all the way under the lattice with a flashlight to coax him out. He was just a tiny thing, shivering and wet. We wrapped him in one of my good guest towels—the ones with the embroidered monogram that my mother-in-law gave us for our wedding. She was so particular about her linens, that woman. If she knew I'd used her wedding gift to dry off a stray cat, she would have had an absolute fit! She used to iron her pillowcases, can you imagine? Who on earth has the time to iron pillowcases? I certainly never did, I was always too busy chasing after the children or tending to the garden. My tomatoes that year were just bursting on the vine, big as softballs... 

But... oh, I've done it again, haven't I? What was it you asked? The exact year? To be perfectly honest, I'm not entirely sure right this second. I'd have to go look at that kitten calendar. They all just sort of blend together when you aren't writing out checks every day!

No, no, I wouldn't say so. I remember what I had for breakfast this morning—a lovely bowl of oatmeal with just a touch of brown sugar. My daughter bought me this new kind of brown sugar, it comes in a resealable pouch which is just marvelous. \n\nThe old boxes always used to dry out so terribly, didn't they? You'd go to bake a batch of cookies and the sugar would be hard as a rock! My mother used to put a slice of apple in the jar to keep it soft. She was always baking, my mother. Every Sunday after church, she'd have a fresh pie cooling on the windowsill. Apple, cherry, rhubarb... oh, the rhubarb we grew in the backyard was just enormous. The leaves were the size of elephant ears! My brother Tommy used to hide under them when we played hide and seek. He was always getting into mischief, that boy. Once he climbed all the way up the big oak tree in the front yard and wouldn't come down until supper time. \n\nBut, oh dear, I'm rambling again, aren't I? What did you ask me? Forgetting recent things? No, I don't think I have any trouble with that. Everything seems perfectly clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble managing your bills?\n\nPatient: Bills? Oh, no, I don't have any trouble with those. My daughter Sarah comes over every other Tuesday and we sit at the kitchen table and go through the mail together. She brings these lovely little pastries from that bakery down on Main Street... the one that used to be the old hardware store? \n\nMr. Henderson ran that hardware store for fifty years, I swear. He always had a jar of peppermint candies on the counter for the children. Real peppermint, you know, the kind that would make your tongue tingle. You just can't find candies like that anymore. Everything now is all artificial flavors and colors. Why, I bought a bag of lemon drops last week and they were bright neon yellow! Like a highlighter pen! I couldn't bring myself to eat them, so I put them out in a dish for the mailman, but he hasn't touched them either. Poor man, he's always rushing these days. They give them these enormous routes now, not like when Henry used to deliver our mail. Henry knew everyone's name, and he'd always stop to ask about my hydrangeas. \n\nOh, but... what were we talking about? The bills? Yes, no trouble at all. Sarah makes sure everything gets paid right on time.\n\nDoctor: What year is it right now?\n\nPatient: The year? Oh, goodness, the years just fly by so fast now, don't they? I was just looking at the new calendar Sarah hung up in the kitchen for me. She always gets me the ones with the baby animals. This one has the most precious little kittens on it. \n\nI had a kitten once, a little tuxedo cat named Sylvester. We found him under the porch during a terrible thunderstorm. My husband had to crawl all the way under the lattice with a flashlight to coax him out. He was just a tiny thing, shivering and wet. We wrapped him in one of my good guest towels—the ones with the embroidered monogram that my mother-in-law gave us for our wedding. She was so particular about her linens, that woman. If she knew I'd used her wedding gift to dry off a stray cat, she would have had an absolute fit! She used to iron her pillowcases, can you imagine? Who on earth has the time to iron pillowcases? I certainly never did, I was always too busy chasing after the children or tending to the garden. My tomatoes that year were just bursting on the vine, big as softballs... \n\nBut... oh, I've done it again, haven't I? What was it you asked? The exact year? To be perfectly honest, I'm not entirely sure right this second. I'd have to go look at that kitten calendar. They all just sort of blend together when you aren't writing out checks every day!\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you tell me where we are right now?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Where we are? Well, we're in this lovely little room, aren't we? It's quite comfortable, really. That chair you're sitting in looks just like the one my husband bought for his study back in... oh, it must have been 1978. 

He was so proud of that chair. It was genuine leather, a deep burgundy color. He used to sit in it every evening with his pipe and the evening paper. The smell of that pipe tobacco... cherry blend, it was. It would fill the whole house. I know smoking isn't good for you—my granddaughter is a nurse and she's always lecturing me about it, even though I've never smoked a day in my life! She works over at the big hospital on the east side of town. The one with the new maternity ward? She says the babies there are just precious. I told her she ought to be having one of her own instead of just looking after everyone else's, but you know how young people are today. Career this, career that. She's talking about going back to school to be a practitioner of some sort. I can't keep all these new titles straight. When I was a girl, a nurse was a nurse and a doctor was a doctor. Old Dr. Gable used to make house calls, carrying that little black bag of his. He gave me a lollipop once when I had the measles... 

Oh dear me, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? You asked where we are. I know I'm at the doctor's office, but as for the exact name of the building or the street... well, Sarah drove me, so I didn't have to pay much mind to the directions.

He always had a jar of peppermint candies on the counter for the children. Real peppermint, you know, the kind that would make your tongue tingle. You just can't find candies like that anymore. Everything now is all artificial flavors and colors. Why, I bought a bag of lemon drops last week and they were bright neon yellow! Like a highlighter pen! I couldn't bring myself to eat them, so I put them out in a dish for the mailman, but he hasn't touched them either. Poor man, he's always rushing these days. They give them these enormous routes now, not like when Henry used to deliver our mail. Henry knew everyone's name, and he'd always stop to ask about my hydrangeas. \n\nOh, but... what were we talking about? The bills? Yes, no trouble at all. Sarah makes sure everything gets paid right on time.\n\nDoctor: What year is it right now?\n\nPatient: The year? Oh, goodness, the years just fly by so fast now, don't they? I was just looking at the new calendar Sarah hung up in the kitchen for me. She always gets me the ones with the baby animals. This one has the most precious little kittens on it. \n\nI had a kitten once, a little tuxedo cat named Sylvester. We found him under the porch during a terrible thunderstorm. My husband had to crawl all the way under the lattice with a flashlight to coax him out. He was just a tiny thing, shivering and wet. We wrapped him in one of my good guest towels—the ones with the embroidered monogram that my mother-in-law gave us for our wedding. She was so particular about her linens, that woman. If she knew I'd used her wedding gift to dry off a stray cat, she would have had an absolute fit! She used to iron her pillowcases, can you imagine? Who on earth has the time to iron pillowcases? I certainly never did, I was always too busy chasing after the children or tending to the garden. My tomatoes that year were just bursting on the vine, big as softballs... \n\nBut... oh, I've done it again, haven't I? What was it you asked? The exact year? To be perfectly honest, I'm not entirely sure right this second. I'd have to go look at that kitten calendar. They all just sort of blend together when you aren't writing out checks every day!\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me where we are right now?\n\nPatient: Where we are? Well, we're in this lovely little room, aren't we? It's quite comfortable, really. That chair you're sitting in looks just like the one my husband bought for his study back in... oh, it must have been 1978. \n\nHe was so proud of that chair. It was genuine leather, a deep burgundy color. He used to sit in it every evening with his pipe and the evening paper. The smell of that pipe tobacco... cherry blend, it was. It would fill the whole house. I know smoking isn't good for you—my granddaughter is a nurse and she's always lecturing me about it, even though I've never smoked a day in my life! She works over at the big hospital on the east side of town. The one with the new maternity ward? She says the babies there are just precious. I told her she ought to be having one of her own instead of just looking after everyone else's, but you know how young people are today. Career this, career that. She's talking about going back to school to be a practitioner of some sort. I can't keep all these new titles straight. When I was a girl, a nurse was a nurse and a doctor was a doctor. Old Dr. Gable used to make house calls, carrying that little black bag of his. He gave me a lollipop once when I had the measles... \n\nOh dear me, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? You asked where we are. I know I'm at the doctor's office, but as for the exact name of the building or the street... well, Sarah drove me, so I didn't have to pay much mind to the directions."
  }
]

Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nOh, heavens no. I still need my sleep, just like always. By the time evening rolls around, I'm usually quite ready for bed. I have this wonderful mattress, you see. We bought it... oh, it must have been fifteen years ago now, from the Sears over at the mall. \n\nIt's such a shame what happened to Sears, isn't it? I used to buy all my daughter's school clothes there when she was little. You could get those sturdy corduroy pants that would last through absolutely everything, not like the flimsy things they sell nowadays. And their appliances! My sister Martha had a Kenmore washing machine that lasted her thirty years. Thirty years! Can you imagine? Now you buy a toaster and you're lucky if it makes it to Christmas. \n\nBut... oh, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, yes, my sleep. No, I don't need less sleep at all. Everything nowadays is made of that stretchy material and it just doesn't breathe at all in the summer heat. We had a terrible heatwave that year, the year of the Fair. Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. He always had a jar of peppermint candies on the counter for the children. Real peppermint, you know, the kind that would make your tongue tingle. You just can't find candies like that anymore. Everything now is all artificial flavors and colors. Why, I bought a bag of lemon drops last week and they were bright neon yellow! Like a highlighter pen! I couldn't bring myself to eat them, so I put them out in a dish for the mailman, but he hasn't touched them either. Poor man, he's always rushing these days. They give them these enormous routes now, not like when Henry used to deliver our mail. Henry knew everyone's name, and he'd always stop to ask about my hydrangeas. \n\nOh, but... what were we talking about? The bills? Yes, no trouble at all. Sarah makes sure everything gets paid right on time.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nThe year? Oh, goodness, the years just fly by so fast now, don't they? I was just looking at the new calendar Sarah hung up in the kitchen for me. She always gets me the ones with the baby animals. This one has the most precious little kittens on it. \n\nI had a kitten once, a little tuxedo cat named Sylvester. We found him under the porch during a terrible thunderstorm. My husband had to crawl all the way under the lattice with a flashlight to coax him out. He was just a tiny thing, shivering and wet. We wrapped him in one of my good guest towels—the ones with the embroidered monogram that my mother-in-law gave us for our wedding. She was so particular about her linens, that woman. If she knew I'd used her wedding gift to dry off a stray cat, she would have had an absolute fit! She used to iron her pillowcases, can you imagine? Who on earth has the time to iron pillowcases? I certainly never did, I was always too busy chasing after the children or tending to the garden. My tomatoes that year were just bursting on the vine, big as softballs... \n\nBut... oh, I've done it again, haven't I? What was it you asked? The exact year? Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.
